\displaystyle:=\|W_{G}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\mathrm{rad}}^{(k)}([0,\tau])}^{2}-\sup_{0\leq s\leq\tau}E_{\mathrm{rad}}^{(k)}[W_{G}](s)+\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(k)}[u_{G}](0,\tau),

(4.163)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{Max},2}^{(k)}[G](0,\tau)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}_{2,R}^{(k)}[u_{G}](0,\tau),

(4.164)

then

\displaystyle\|G\|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}([0,\tau])}^{2}\simeq\sup_{0\leq s\leq\tau}E_{\mathrm{Max}}^{(k)}[G](s)

(4.165)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}[G](0,\tau)+\sup_{0\leq s\leq\tau}\mathcal{E}_{2,R}^{(k)}[u_{G}](s)+\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{Max},2}^{(k)}[G](0,\tau).

(4.166)

In particular,
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
dominates the non–degenerate slice energy of
G
, a local spacetime bulk through the horizon/trapping/far regions, and the outgoing
r^{2}
hierarchy controlling the radiation field at null infinity.

3.

Reconstruction estimate in final form.
Combining (
4.161
) with Proposition
4.48
yields (
1.5
) with
\ell_{\mathrm{rec}}=0
.

Proof.

If
\|G\|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}([0,\tau])}=0
, then
\|W_{G}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\mathrm{rad}}^{(k)}([0,\tau])}=0
. Hence
E_{\mathrm{rad}}^{(k)}[W_{G}](s)=0
for every
s\in[0,\tau]
, so
W_{G}\equiv 0
on the slab. Lemma
4.42
therefore gives
G\equiv 0
. Thus (
4.161
) is a genuine norm.

The exact radiative triple is algebraic in
G
:
W_{G}=\mathfrak{B}_{\mathrm{NP}}(G)
. The associated master variable is first order in
W_{G}
:
u_{G}=\mathfrak{D}_{M}W_{G}
. Consequently the map

G\longmapsto(W_{G},u_{G})

is a linear differential operator of order one with smooth stationary–axisymmetric coefficients, while Lemma
4.42
gives the inverse algebraic recovery
G=\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{NP}}(W_{G})
. Commuting with the symmetry algebra and with the tangential derivatives defining the norms therefore shows that
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
is equivalent to a weighted tensorial
H^{1}
–type physical–space norm of
G
. This proves item (1).

Item (2) is simply a repackaging of (
4.158
): the first term is the radiative slice energy plus the local spacetime transport/elliptic bulk, while the second term is the full master energy/Morawetz/
r^{p}
package of Step 1. The equivalence (
4.165
) follows immediately from Definitions (
4.158
) and (
4.161
) and from the concrete formulas (
4.162
)–(
4.164
). Since
E_{\mathrm{Max}}^{(k)}
is algebraically equivalent to the slice
L^{2}
norm of
G
, since
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
contains both the recovered radiative bulk and the master Morawetz bulk, and since
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{Max},2}^{(k)}
is exactly the outgoing Step 10
r^{2}
radiation flux of the associated master variable,
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
has the stated physical–space content.

Item (3) is Proposition
4.48
rewritten with the final notation (
4.161
). This is exactly Step 5 of Remark
4.38
.
∎

Lemma 4.50

(Polynomial decay of the master energy from the Step 10 package)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
1.8
. Then for every homogeneous master solution
u
, every integer
k\geq 0
, and every
\tau\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}^{(k)}[u](\tau)+\mathcal{E}_{2,R}^{(k)}[u](\tau)\leq C_{k}(1+\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{C},0}^{(k+2)}[u](0).

(4.167)

In particular the commuted non–degenerate energy and the commuted weighted far–field energy of
u
tend to zero as
\tau\to\infty
.

Proof.

This is the standard Dafermos–Rodnianski
r^{p}
iteration applied to the Step 10 hierarchy furnished by Theorem
1.8
; see, e.g.,
[
9
,
10
]
. The
p=2
estimate controls the time integral of the
p=1
flux on dyadic slabs. A pigeonhole argument therefore produces a dyadic sequence on which the
p=1
flux decays like
O(\tau^{-1})
. Reinserting that estimate into the
p=1
hierarchy gives
p=0
decay one order lower, and the Step 8 redshift/Morawetz estimate recovers the full non–degenerate energy from the decaying weighted flux. Since the paper only needs a polynomial rate, the displayed
O((1+\tau)^{-1})
bound is sufficient. Commuting with
Z^{I}
gives the same estimate at order
k
.
∎

Step 6: close the Sobolev argument.

Once the tensorial norm of Step 5 is fixed, the remaining issue in item (2) of Theorem
1.9
is pointwise decay. This is a standard combination of local Sobolev on the compact region, one–dimensional Sobolev plus sphere Sobolev in the far field, and the master decay estimate above.

Proposition 4.51

(Step 6 in the proof of Theorem
1.9
: Sobolev closure and polynomial pointwise decay)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
1.8
. Let
G
be a smooth charge–free Maxwell field. Then for every integer
k\geq 4
and every
\tau\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{|I|_{*}\leq k-4}\|Z^{I}G\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}^{2}\leq C_{k}(1+\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}[G](0).

(4.168)

Consequently
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
contains enough local spacetime control to yield polynomial pointwise decay of
G
and its commuted derivatives.

Proof.

Fix
R>r_{+}
large.

On the compact region
\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}
, the slices have uniformly bounded geometry. Standard Sobolev therefore gives

\displaystyle\sum_{|I|_{*}\leq k-4}\|Z^{I}G\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\})}^{2}\leq C_{k}\sum_{|J|_{*}\leq k-2}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|\nabla_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{j}Z^{J}G\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}.

By Proposition
4.49
, the right–hand side is controlled by the slice component of
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
. Proposition
4.48
, the homogeneous master estimate (
1.3
) with
\mathcal{G}=0
, Proposition
4.45
, and Lemma
4.50
therefore yield

\sum_{|I|_{*}\leq k-4}\|Z^{I}G\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\})}^{2}\leq C_{k}(1+\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}[G](0).

On the far region
r\geq R
, write
W_{G}=(\psi_{+},\phi_{1},\psi_{-})
. For each component
w
of
W_{G}
and each commutator
Z^{I}
with
|I|_{*}\leq k-4
, the one–dimensional Sobolev inequality in
v
, together with Sobolev on
S^{2}
, controls
r\,Z^{I}w
pointwise by the far–field weighted part of the norm
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
. By Propositions
4.48
and
4.49
, that weighted part is bounded by the master norm of
u_{G}
, and Lemma
4.50
supplies the same
O((1+\tau)^{-1})
decay. Hence

\sum_{|I|_{*}\leq k-4}\sup_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}|Z^{I}W_{G}|^{2}\leq C_{k}(1+\tau)^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}[G](0).

Since
G=\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{NP}}(W_{G})
and the coefficients of
\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{NP}}
are smooth and bounded on the exterior, the same estimate holds for
G
. Combining the compact and far–field bounds proves (
4.168
). This is exactly Step 6 of Remark
4.38
.
∎

Step 7: construct the master radiation fields.

At this point item (2) of Theorem
1.9
is finished. To prove the scattering clauses one first fixes the Cauchy and asymptotic Hilbert spaces for the master equation.

Definition 4.52

(Master Cauchy and asymptotic data spaces)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{M,0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})
be the Hilbert completion of smooth compactly supported initial master data with norm

\displaystyle\|(u,\nabla_{n}u)|_{\Sigma_{0}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{M,0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}:=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{C},0}^{(k)}[u](0).

(4.169)

For a smooth homogeneous master solution
u
, let
\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{\pm}[u]
denote its future/past radiation fields on
\mathcal{I}^{\pm}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
. Let
\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k),\mathrm{sm}}
be the set of such smooth traces, equipped with the flux norm induced by the Step 10
r^{2}
hierarchy and the horizon
N
–flux. Define
\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}
to be the Hilbert completion of
\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k),\mathrm{sm}}
in that norm.

Proposition 4.53

(Step 7 in the proof of Theorem
1.9
: bounded master Cauchy–to–radiation map)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
1.8
. Then for every integer
k\geq 0
the future and past trace maps

\mathscr{S}_{M,0}^{\pm}:\mathcal{H}_{M,0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}

are well defined and bounded.

Proof.

For smooth solutions, existence of the horizon trace is part of the non–degenerate energy estimate and existence of the null–infinity trace is exactly the radiation control contained in the Step 10
r^{2}
hierarchy. The corresponding flux norm is bounded by the left–hand side of (
1.3
) with
\mathcal{G}=0
, hence by
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{C},0}^{(k)}[u](0)
. This proves boundedness on the smooth class. The trace maps therefore extend by density to the Hilbert completion
\mathcal{H}_{M,0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})
. The past map is obtained from the future one by time reversal of the stationary homogeneous master equation. This is exactly Step 7 of Remark
4.38
.
∎

Step 8: solve the master final–state problem.

The bounded trace maps from Step 7, together with the Step 10 physical–space decay package, place the master equation in the standard abstract scattering framework for short–range linear waves on stationary black–hole exteriors.

Proposition 4.54

(Step 8 in the proof of Theorem
1.9
: master scattering isomorphism and final–state problem)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
1.8
. Then the bounded operators
\mathscr{S}_{M,0}^{\pm}
from Proposition
4.53
are isomorphisms

\mathscr{S}_{M,0}^{\pm}:\mathcal{H}_{M,0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}.

Equivalently, for each sign
\pm
and each asymptotic datum
\mathfrak{a}^{\pm}\in\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}
there exists a unique finite–energy homogeneous master solution
u
such that
\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{\pm}[u]=\mathfrak{a}^{\pm}
, and

\displaystyle\|(u,\nabla_{n}u)|_{\Sigma_{0}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{M,0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})}\leq C_{k}\|\mathfrak{a}^{\pm}\|_{\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}}.

(4.170)

Proof.

By Proposition
4.53
, the trace maps are bounded on the master Cauchy space. By Lemma
4.50
, the homogeneous master evolution has the standard local decay input needed in the physical–space construction of wave operators, and the equation is linear, stationary, and time reversible. The usual black–hole scattering argument for short–range waves therefore applies verbatim: one truncates smooth asymptotic data at large advanced/retarded times, solves the corresponding backward homogeneous Cauchy problems, uses the Step 10 boundedness to obtain a Cauchy sequence in
\mathcal{H}_{M,0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})
, and then passes to the limit. Uniqueness follows by applying the same argument to zero asymptotic data. Thus the wave operators exist and are bounded inverses to the trace maps. See the physical–space scattering constructions underlying
[
9
,
10
]
. This is exactly Step 8 of Remark
4.38
.
∎

Step 9: asymptotic reconstruction of the Maxwell radiation fields.

The finite–time reconstruction system of Step 2 is stationary, uniformly regular up to the horizon, and short range at infinity. Therefore its boundary limit defines bounded operators on the asymptotic data spaces.

Proposition 4.55

(Step 9 in the proof of Theorem
1.9
: asymptotic reconstruction and asymptotic master reduction)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
1.8
. Then for each sign
\pm
there exist Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{Max},\pm}^{(k)}
of charge–free Maxwell asymptotic data and bounded linear maps

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\infty}^{\pm}:\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{Max},\pm}^{(k)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\infty}^{\pm}:\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{Max},\pm}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)},

such that:

1.

on smooth solutions,
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}^{\pm}
is obtained by taking the
\mathcal{I}^{\pm}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
traces of the exact radiative triple reconstructed in Proposition
4.43
and then recovering the Maxwell radiation fields algebraically;

2.

on smooth solutions,
\mathcal{T}_{\infty}^{\pm}
is the boundary limit of the first–order master reduction
u_{G}=\mathfrak{D}_{M}W_{G}
;

3.

\mathcal{T}_{\infty}^{\pm}\circ\mathcal{R}_{\infty}^{\pm}=I
on
\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}^{\pm}\circ\mathcal{T}_{\infty}^{\pm}=I
on
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{Max},\pm}^{(k)}
;

4.

the maps are consistent with finite–time reconstruction in the sense that, for every charge–free Maxwell field
G
with associated master solution
u_{G}
,

\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{Max}}^{\pm}[G]=\mathcal{R}_{\infty}^{\pm}\big(\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{\pm}[u_{G}]\big).

In particular the master and charge–free Maxwell asymptotic data spaces are canonically isomorphic.

Proof.

On the dense class of smooth finite–energy solutions, apply Proposition
4.43
on the slabs
[0,\tau]
and pass to the limit
\tau\to\pm\infty
. Because the transport fields are stationary and uniformly transversal, because the sphere elliptic operators remain uniformly invertible on the mean–zero sector, and because all coefficients are short range, the boundary traces of the reconstructed variables depend continuously on the boundary traces of the master solution. This defines the smooth asymptotic reconstruction map. The boundary limit of the first–order operator
u_{G}=\mathfrak{D}_{M}W_{G}
gives the inverse smooth map
\mathcal{T}_{\infty}^{\pm}
. Since these two maps are mutual inverses on the smooth class, they extend by continuity to the Hilbert completions
\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{Max},\pm}^{(k)}
, still as mutual inverses. The last item is simply the statement that taking the asymptotic trace commutes with the finite–time reconstruction system. This is exactly Step 9 of Remark
4.38
.
∎

Step 10: transfer scattering from the master system to Maxwell.

Definition 4.56

(Charge–free Maxwell Cauchy space)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})
be the Hilbert completion of smooth charge–free Maxwell data with norm

\displaystyle\|G|_{\Sigma_{0}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}:=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}[G](0).

(4.171)

Proposition 4.57

(Step 10 in the proof of Theorem
1.9
: Maxwell scattering and final–state problem)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
1.8
. Then for each sign
\pm
the charge–free Maxwell Cauchy–to–radiation maps

\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{\pm}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{Max},\pm}^{(k)}

are bounded isomorphisms. Equivalently, the charge–free Maxwell final–state problem on
\mathcal{I}^{\pm}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
is well posed.

Proof.

For smooth Maxwell data
G|_{\Sigma_{0}}
, define
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{\pm}G:=\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{Max}}^{\pm}[G]
. By Proposition
4.45
, the initial master reduction map

G|_{\Sigma_{0}}\longmapsto u_{G}|_{\Sigma_{0}}

is bounded from
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})
to
\mathcal{H}_{M,0}^{(k)}(\Sigma_{0})
. By Propositions
4.54
and
4.55
,

\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{\pm}=\mathcal{R}_{\infty}^{\pm}\circ\mathscr{S}_{M,0}^{\pm}\circ\mathcal{T}_{0},

where
\mathcal{T}_{0}
denotes the bounded initial master reduction. Hence
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{\pm}
is bounded.

To prove injectivity, suppose
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{\pm}G=0
. Applying
\mathcal{T}_{\infty}^{\pm}
and using Proposition
4.55
gives
\mathscr{S}_{M,0}^{\pm}(u_{G})=0
. Proposition
4.54
therefore yields
u_{G}=0
. Proposition
4.48
then forces
G=0
. Thus
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{\pm}
is injective.

Finally,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{Max},\pm}^{(k)}
is by Proposition
4.55
exactly the image of
\mathcal{R}_{M,\pm}^{(k)}
under the isomorphism
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}^{\pm}
. Since
\mathscr{S}_{M,0}^{\pm}
is onto, the Maxwell map is onto as well. Its inverse is the composition of the bounded inverses from Propositions
4.54
and
4.55
. This is exactly Step 10 of Remark
4.38
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.9
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
1.8
. Item (1) is Proposition
4.40
. Item (2) is completed by Propositions
4.43
–
4.51
, with no derivative loss and with
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
given by (
4.161
). Item (3) is Propositions
4.53
and
4.54
. Item (4) is Propositions
4.55
and
4.57
. This is exactly the content of Propositions
4.40
–
4.57
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.10
.

By Proposition
3.1
, the stationary subtraction

F_{\mathrm{rad}}=F-F_{\mathrm{stat}}^{\mathrm{KN}}\big(q_{E}[F],q_{B}[F]\big)

is charge–free. Let
u_{M}
be the associated master variable given by Proposition
4.1
; since
F
is sourceless,
u_{M}
satisfies

\big(\Box_{g_{\mathrm{KN}}}+\mathcal{V}_{M}\big)u_{M}=0.

Applying item (1) of Theorem
1.9
with
\mathcal{G}=0
and then item (2) gives

\displaystyle\|F_{\mathrm{rad}}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}([0,\tau])}^{2}\leq C_{k}\Big(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}[F_{\mathrm{rad}}](0)+\|u_{M}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{M,0}^{(k)}([0,\tau])}^{2}\Big)\leq C_{k}\,

\displaystyle\qquad\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}[F_{\mathrm{rad}}](0),

which is exactly (
1.6
). The physical–space content of this estimate is the first three bullets of the theorem, and the polynomial pointwise decay is the Sobolev conclusion in item (2), made quantitative in Proposition
4.51
. The scattering statement is item (4) of Theorem
1.9
. Adding back the stationary family
F_{\mathrm{stat}}^{\mathrm{KN}}\big(q_{E}[F],q_{B}[F]\big)
recovers the unique decomposition of any finite–energy sourceless Maxwell field into stationary and radiative parts. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1.11
.

Under the charge–compatibility hypothesis, the chosen subtraction places
F_{\mathrm{rad}}
in the charge–free sector. Let
u_{M}
be the associated master variable from Proposition
4.1
; then

\big(\Box_{g_{\mathrm{KN}}}+\mathcal{V}_{M}\big)u_{M}=\mathcal{S}_{M}(\mathcal{J}).

Applying item (1) of Theorem
1.9
and then item (2) gives

\displaystyle\|F_{\mathrm{rad}}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}([0,\tau])}^{2}\leq C_{k}\Big(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}[F_{\mathrm{rad}}](0)+\int_{0}^{\tau}\!\!\|\mathcal{S}_{M}(\mathcal{J})\|_{\mathcal{N}_{0}^{*}}^{2}(\sigma)\,d\sigma\Big),

which is exactly (
1.8
). Since
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{Max},0}^{(k)}
contains the tensorial non–degenerate energy, Morawetz bulk, outgoing
r^{p}
hierarchy, and the local spacetime control needed for the Sobolev estimate, the stated forced physical–space and pointwise bounds follow immediately. The last sentence is simply the same estimate taken on the interval
[0,\tau]
for arbitrary
\tau
.
∎

Remark 4.58

(What is special to the massless theory)
.

The Maxwell field has gauge invariance and two conserved asymptotic charges in the sourceless case, namely the electric and magnetic charges. Because of this, decay and scattering are never stated directly for the full field
F
but only for its radiative part after subtraction of the stationary two–charge Coulomb family. This phenomenon is unique to the massless gauge field and has no direct analogue in the massive Proca theory.

5
Discussion and broader context

The logic of the paper is therefore completely explicit. The structural input is subtraction of the stationary electric–magnetic two–charge family together with the short–range reduction theorem, Theorem
1.1
, whose scalar specialization is (
4.1
). Proposition
4.20
then derives the one–level inhomogeneous redshift–Morawetz estimate (
4.89
) from the order–zero Step 6 multiplier input, and Proposition
4.27
proves the one–level far–field weighted estimate (
4.112
). These feed into Theorem
1.8
, which is now conditional only through that single remaining Step 6 input. Propositions
4.35
and
4.36
verify the homogeneous radiative package on the two fully understood edges of parameter space, namely the full sub–extremal Reissner–Nordström family
a=0
,
0<|Q|<M
and the full sub–extremal Kerr family
Q=0
. Theorem
3.10
then supplies the currently rigorous interior Kerr–Newman verifications of the same remaining input: its axisymmetric part, combined with Theorem
1.9
, yields the unconditional slowly rotating strongly charged Maxwell theorem, Theorem
1.13
, on the whole relative regime
|a|\ll|Q|<M
, while its small–parameter part yields the non–axisymmetric Theorem
1.15
on the full doubly small regime
|a|,|Q|\ll M
. Beyond these regimes, once the Step 6 input of Theorem
1.8
is granted, Theorem
1.9
supplies the quantitative reconstruction and scattering theory, Theorem
1.10
upgrades this to full tensorial decay and scattering, and Corollary
1.11
yields the sourced tensorial estimates.

Remark 5.1

(Literature status beyond the introductory overview)
.

For the fixed-background Maxwell equation, two unconditional edges of parameter space are already available. In the charged but non-rotating case, Sterbenz and Tataru prove local energy decay for Maxwell on generic spherically symmetric black–hole backgrounds, which in particular covers the full sub–extremal Reissner–Nordström family and underlies Proposition
4.35

[
1
]
. In the uncharged rotating case, Benomio and Teixeira da Costa give a recent full sub–extremal Kerr physical–space theory with a two–charge stationary decomposition, which underlies Proposition
4.36

[
2
]
. For the scalar wave equation on full sub–extremal Kerr–Newman, Civin proved a non–degenerate energy bound together with a trapping–degenerate integrated local energy decay estimate obtained through phase–space analysis
[
12
]
; this is the scalar model behind the consistency warning in Remark
4.11
. On rotating charged Kerr–Newman, Giorgi derived the coupled Teukolsky and generalized Regge–Wheeler equations for electromagnetic–gravitational perturbations
[
3
]
, proved that the Carter operator still commutes with the wave operator and hence fits into an Andersson–Blue physical–space framework
[
4
]
, Hollands and Toomani constructed radiation–gauge potentials with source
[
5
]
, and Giorgi together with Wan obtained boundedness and decay results in small–parameter and axially symmetric regimes
[
6
,
7
]
. These are key ingredients, but they do not by themselves yet imply the remaining non–degenerate Step 6 input, and hence the full fixed-background charge–free Maxwell package considered in Theorem
1.8
, for arbitrary sub–extremal rotating charged parameters.

Remark 5.2

(Central open problem)
.

The main unresolved problem isolated by Theorem
1.10
is now sharply localized. On the rotating charged side, Theorem
1.13
already gives the full tensorial decay and scattering theorem on the whole relative regime
|a|\ll|Q|<M
in axial symmetry, and Theorem
1.15
gives the corresponding non–axisymmetric theorem on the full doubly small regime
|a|,|Q|\ll M
. The reconstruction and scattering stage is now complete: Remark
4.38
, Propositions
4.40
–
4.57
, and Theorem
1.9
show that once the radiative master package is known, the full tensorial Maxwell decay and scattering theorem follows with no derivative loss. After Propositions
4.20
and
4.27
, the remaining missing step on the full rotating charged family is therefore no longer the pair of order–zero hypotheses (
4.89
) and (
4.112
); it is the order–zero Step 6 Morawetz multiplier input in the non–axisymmetric, strongly charged regime
0<|a|\ll M
,
0<|Q|<M
,
|Q|=O(M)
. Concretely, this means establishing a homogeneous radiative Kerr–Newman multiplier estimate strong enough to generate the non–degenerate energy, Morawetz/ILED, and hence the higher–order package from which the full tensorial theorem then follows automatically.

Appendix A
Mathematical problem behind the Step 6 framework

The appendix has three purposes. First, it records precisely why the order–zero Step 6 seed (
4.46
) remains external on the full non–axisymmetric rotating charged family. Second, it notes that the short–range master potential is perturbative once the corresponding geometric wave estimate is available. Third, it checks that, once such a Step 6 seed is granted in the broader reference framework, the later one–level estimates fit together without any logical circularity.

A.1
Why the full–range Step 6 seed remains external

We isolate first the geometric obstruction. Let

p(x,\xi):=g_{\mathrm{KN}}^{\mu\nu}(x)\,\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\nu}

be the principal symbol of
\Box_{g_{\mathrm{KN}}}
and let
H_{p}
be the associated Hamilton vector field on
T^{*}\mathcal{D}
. For a real first–order multiplier
X
with symbol
G_{X}(x,\xi):=X^{\mu}(x)\xi_{\mu}
, the principal symbol of the quadratic bulk coming from the stress–energy current is

\sigma\!\left(\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{T}^{\mu\nu}[v]\pi^{X}_{\mu\nu}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\,\{p,G_{X}\}=\frac{1}{2}\,H_{p}G_{X}

on the characteristic set
\{p=0\}
, while the
w
–correction in (
4.43
) is lower order and does not change this principal part.

Proposition A.1

(Radial first–order currents are trapping–degenerate)
.

Let
X=f(r)\partial_{r}
with
f
smooth and bounded, and let
w
be any bounded real weight. Let
\gamma=(x,\xi)
be a trapped null covector for the Kerr–Newman geodesic flow, so that
p(\gamma)=0
and the bicharacteristic through
\gamma
stays in a compact
r
–region. Then the principal symbol of the bulk density
\mathcal{K}^{X,w}
vanishes at
\gamma
:

\sigma(\mathcal{K}^{X,w})(\gamma)=0.

Consequently no estimate obtained solely from integrating the corrected current
\mathcal{J}^{X,w}
can yield a spacetime bulk whose principal part is pointwise comparable to
|\nabla v|^{2}
on a full neighborhood of the trapped set.

Proof.

For
X=f(r)\partial_{r}
one has
G_{X}=f(r)\,\xi_{r}
, so on
\{p=0\}
the principal quadratic form generated by the stress–energy part of the current is

\frac{1}{2}\,H_{p}(f(r)\xi_{r})=\frac{1}{2}\Big(f^{\prime}(r)\,(H_{p}r)\,\xi_{r}+f(r)\,H_{p}\xi_{r}\Big).

If
\gamma
lies on the trapped set, then the null bicharacteristic through
\gamma
has constant radial position to first order, hence
H_{p}r(\gamma)=0
. Trapping also means that the radial momentum is critical there, so
H_{p}\xi_{r}(\gamma)=0
. Therefore

\frac{1}{2}\,H_{p}(f(r)\xi_{r})(\gamma)=0.

The weight
w
contributes only lower–order terms and does not alter the principal symbol at the level of first derivatives. Hence
\sigma(\mathcal{K}^{X,w})(\gamma)=0
. This rules out a non–degenerate order–zero bulk from the raw radial first–order current alone.
∎

Proposition A.2

(Short–range potentials are perturbative at Step 6)
.

Assume there exist a stationary timelike multiplier
N
, a real stationary axisymmetric vector field
X
, a real stationary axisymmetric weight
w
, and constants
\delta,c,C>0
such that every smooth solution of

\Box_{g_{\mathrm{KN}}}v=H

satisfies

\displaystyle\delta\Big(E_{N}[v](\tau_{2})+F_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}^{N}[v](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\Big)+c\Big(\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{loc}}[v](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})+\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{far}}[v](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\Big)

(A.1)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq CE_{N}[v](\tau_{1})+\int_{\mathcal{D}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\Re\Big((Xv+\tfrac{1}{2}wv)\,\overline{H}\Big).

Suppose moreover that
\mathcal{V}_{M}
is smooth, stationary, axisymmetric, bounded on
\{r<R_{\mathrm{erg}}\}
, and satisfies
|\mathcal{V}_{M}|\leq Cr^{-3}
on
\{r\geq R_{\mathrm{erg}}\}
. Then every smooth solution of

(\Box_{g_{\mathrm{KN}}}+\mathcal{V}_{M})v=H

obeys the same estimate, with the same left–hand side and with modified constants depending only on the background bounds for
X
,
w
, and
\mathcal{V}_{M}
.

Proof.

Apply (
A.1
) with source
H-\mathcal{V}_{M}v
. The additional contribution is

-\int_{\mathcal{D}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\Re\Big((Xv+\tfrac{1}{2}wv)\,\overline{\mathcal{V}_{M}v}\Big).

Because
X
and
w
are bounded, one has
|Xv+\tfrac{1}{2}wv|\leq C(|\nabla v|+|v|)
. On the compact region
\{r_{+}<r<R_{\mathrm{erg}}\}
this gives

\big|(Xv+\tfrac{1}{2}wv)\,\mathcal{V}_{M}v\big|\leq\eta\big(|\nabla v|^{2}+|v|^{2}\big)

after enlarging the constant. On the far region
\{r\geq R_{\mathrm{erg}}\}
the decay
|\mathcal{V}_{M}|\leq Cr^{-3}
yields

\big|(Xv+\tfrac{1}{2}wv)\,\mathcal{V}_{M}v\big|\leq C_{\eta}r^{-3}|v|^{2}+\eta r^{-3}|\nabla v|^{2}.

Choosing
\eta>0
sufficiently small relative to
c
and absorbing both contributions into the left–hand side proves the claim.
∎

A.2
The remaining full–range analytic issue

Proposition
A.2
shows that the short–range master potential is not the source of the full–range difficulty. The remaining issue in the broader reference framework is the following.

Proposition A.3

(Reduction to the geometric wave estimate)
.

If the geometric wave estimate (
A.1
) is available on the full non–axisymmetric rotating charged Kerr–Newman family, then the corresponding Step 6 seed for the short–range master equation follows. Consequently, within the broader conditional template of Theorem
1.8
, the only genuinely missing analytic input is a local–energy estimate for the geometric wave equation strong enough to control
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{loc}}+\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{far}}
through trapping.

Proof.

This is immediate from Proposition
A.2
.
∎

Remark A.4

(Status of the broader framework)
.

The present paper does not attempt the full–range problem isolated above. It only records it in order to keep the broader modular framework logically transparent. In particular, the appendix does not claim a proof of the exact order–zero Step 6 seed (
4.46
) on the full non–axisymmetric sub–extremal Kerr–Newman family.

A.3
No circularity with Steps 8 and 9

Once the Step 6 seed is granted in the broader reference framework, the later one–level estimates fit together without any hidden loop.

Proposition A.5

(Consistency of (
4.89
), (
4.112
), and the Step 6 seed)
.

Fix
0\leq p\leq 2
and
R\geq R_{0}
, where
R_{0}
is the constant from Proposition
4.27
. Let
v
solve

(\Box_{g_{\mathrm{KN}}}+\mathcal{V}_{M})v=H

on
\mathcal{D}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
, and write
\Phi:=rv
on
\{r\geq R\}
.

(i)
If the order–zero Step 6 seed estimate (
4.46
) holds, then (
4.89
) follows. In particular, the one–level redshift–Morawetz estimate is not an additional hypothesis.

(ii)
The proof of (
4.112
) uses only the renormalized far–field equation and the asymptotic short–range bounds; it does not use (
4.89
) or the higher–order Step 8 hierarchy.

(iii)
Whenever both (
4.89
) and (
4.112
) are available for the same
v
, one has the closed combined estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p,R}[\Phi](\tau_{2})+\mathcal{F}_{p,R}[\Phi](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})+\mathcal{B}_{p,R}[\Phi](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

(A.2)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{p}\Big(\mathcal{E}_{p,R}[\Phi](\tau_{1})+E_{N}[v](\tau_{1})+\|H\|_{\mathcal{N}_{M,*}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}^{2}+\|H\|_{\mathcal{N}_{p,R}^{*}}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2}\Big).

(A.3)

Consequently the appearance of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{far}}[v](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
on the right–hand side of (
4.112
) is exactly consistent with Step 6.

Proof.

Part (i) is Proposition
4.20
. Part (ii) follows by inspection of the proof of Proposition
4.27
, which uses only Lemma
4.25
, Lemma
4.26
, the asymptotic short–range coefficient bounds, and the weighted source norm (
4.104
). For part (iii), start from (
4.112
). Since (
4.89
) contains
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{far}}[v](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
as a positive term on its left–hand side, it gives

\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{far}}[v](\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\leq C\Big(E_{N}[v](\tau_{1})+\|H\|_{\mathcal{N}_{M,*}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}^{2}\Big).

Substituting this bound into (
4.112
) yields (
A.2
).
∎

Remark A.6

(Alignment with the present weak theorem package)
.

The final fixed–background theorems of the present paper are restricted to the weak non–axisymmetric regime
0<|a|\leq c_{a}M
,
0<|Q|\leq c_{Q}M
. On that regime the required radiative input is imported from Theorem
3.10
(2), so Theorems
1.2
,
1.3
, and
1.4
are unconditional. The broader full–range Step 6 issue recorded here is kept only as comparison material and plays no role in the proof of the main weak–Kerr–Newman results.
